## [OpenAI unveils first custom AI inference chip 'Jalapeno'](https://techcrunch.com/2026/06/24/openai-unveils-its-first-custom-chip-built-by-broadcom/) ⭐️ 9.0/10

OpenAI, in collaboration with Broadcom and TSMC, has unveiled its first custom AI inference chip named 'Jalapeno', designed specifically for large language model inference and developed from design to production in nine months using OpenAI's own models to accelerate the design process. This marks a significant strategic move by OpenAI to reduce reliance on GPU suppliers like Nvidia, potentially cutting inference costs by roughly 50% and improving performance for its AI services such as ChatGPT. The chip is manufactured by TSMC and shows cost savings of about 50% compared to typical AI GPUs, according to Broadcom CEO Hock Tan. OpenAI used its own AI models to accelerate parts of the chip design and optimization process, a novel approach that has sparked community discussion.

hackernews · jamdesk · Jun 24, 17:47 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48663324)

**Background**: AI inference chips are specialized hardware designed to run trained AI models efficiently, as opposed to training chips which are used to train models. OpenAI has been working to build its own custom chips to reduce dependence on external suppliers and optimize performance for its specific workloads. The use of AI to design chips, known as AI-accelerated chip design, is an emerging field where models help automate and optimize the design process, potentially reducing development time.

<a id="item-2"></a>
## [Unified Geometry of Diffusion and Flow Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24157) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper unifies diffusion models and flow matching as two variational principles on the Wasserstein manifold, showing they are not separate theories but different discretizations of the same geometric structure. This unification provides a deeper theoretical foundation for generative modeling, potentially leading to more efficient algorithms and better understanding of existing methods like DDPM, DDIM, and Flow Matching. Diffusion models correspond to a gradient flow of free energy on the Wasserstein manifold, while flow matching follows a geodesic (optimal transport path). The JKO scheme is the implicit Euler discretization of the gradient flow, recovering DDPM, DDIM, and others.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: The Wasserstein manifold is a geometric structure on the space of probability measures, where the distance is given by optimal transport. The JKO scheme is a time discretization method for gradient flows in this space. The Benamou-Brenier formula characterizes optimal transport as a dynamic problem.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://gavinkerrigan.github.io/notes/analysis/gradient_flows/jko/">The JKO Scheme</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/jko-scheme">JKO Scheme : Gradient Flows in Wasserstein Space</a></li>
<li><a href="https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/benamou-brenier-formulation">Benamou–Brenier Formulation in Optimal Transport</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-3"></a>
## [RaDaR: A 32B Reasoning LLM for Rare Disease Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24510) ⭐️ 9.0/10

Researchers introduced RaDaR, a compact 32B-parameter reasoning LLM for rare disease diagnosis, which outperforms larger models like DeepSeek-R1 (671B) on benchmarks and showed a 21.44 percentage point improvement in physician diagnostic accuracy in a randomized trial. This work addresses the critical challenge of rare disease diagnosis, where timely identification is hindered by scarce expertise, and demonstrates that a specialized, smaller LLM can be both clinically effective and deployable, potentially accelerating diagnosis for millions of patients. RaDaR was trained on 49,170 public free-text cases and 104,666 synthetic cases using reasoning-enhanced training. In a retrospective cohort, it prioritized the final diagnosis before clinical suspicion in 61.06% of cases, with a potential lead time of 1.87 months.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Rare diseases affect millions worldwide but diagnosis is often delayed due to lack of specialized expertise. Large language models (LLMs) have shown promise in medical diagnosis, but existing models are often too large for clinical deployment or lack reasoning capabilities. RaDaR is a compact reasoning LLM designed specifically for this domain, using synthetic data to overcome data scarcity.

<a id="item-4"></a>
## [FP8 Tensor Cores Enable FP64 FFT on Blackwell Ultra](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23698) ⭐️ 9.0/10

This paper introduces the Ozaki-Bailey FFT, an emulated 3-D FFT that uses FP8 tensor cores and Garner reconstruction to achieve FP64-equivalent throughput on NVIDIA Blackwell Ultra GPUs, which have severely reduced native FP64 performance. This work makes Blackwell Ultra viable for full-FP64 FFT through software alone, addressing a critical bottleneck in high-performance computing where FP64 throughput is limited. It could enable scientific applications that rely on double-precision FFTs to run efficiently on next-generation hardware. The method uses Bailey's six-step decomposition with a small inner factor k=32 for N=1024, and Garner reconstruction splits into Phase A (inner products on FP8/INT8 tensor cores) and Phase B (per-output reduction using Kulisch fixed-point arithmetic on INT32 SIMT pipes). The projected runtime is ~18 ms for a 1024^3 FFT at full FP64, close to the 12.9 ms memory roof.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: The Ozaki Scheme II is a framework for emulating high-precision matrix multiplication using only low-precision kernels like INT8 or FP8. Garner's algorithm reconstructs a large integer from its residues modulo several coprime moduli, enabling the emulation of FP64 arithmetic. The Bailey six-step FFT decomposes a large 1-D FFT into smaller ones to improve cache efficiency.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://www.emergentmind.com/topics/ozaki-ii-scheme">Ozaki-II Scheme: Emulating FP64 on Low-Precision Hardware</a></li>
<li><a href="https://cp-algorithms.com/algebra/chinese-remainder-theorem.html">Chinese Remainder Theorem - Algorithms for Competitive Programming</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

<a id="item-5"></a>
## [LLM Safety Fails for Most Mental Health Conditions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23884) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new study evaluates six proprietary LLMs across 16 DSM-5 conditions using four adversarial attack variants, finding that safety safeguards hold reliably only for suicide and self-harm, while conditions like eating disorders and substance use disorder exhibit failure rates up to 100%. This research exposes critical safety gaps in LLMs used for mental health conversations, posing serious risks to vulnerable populations, especially as these models are increasingly integrated into educational settings. The study introduces an eight-dimension harm taxonomy and a multi-dimensional evaluation framework, and tested models include GPT-4, Claude, and Gemini. The results underscore the need for clearly defined harm categories and condition-specific safeguards.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for mental health support, but their safety safeguards are often inadequate. The DSM-5 is the standard classification of mental disorders used by clinicians. Adversarial attacks are inputs designed to bypass model safeguards, and this study uses four variants to probe vulnerabilities.

<a id="item-6"></a>
## [African Language Tokenization Penalty Quantified in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24460) ⭐️ 9.0/10

A new paper systematically measures the tokenization penalty for 20 African languages across 11 frontier and open tokenizers, finding a median 1.88x token count premium over English, with up to 8.92x for N'Ko script. This structural bias leads to up to 8.9x higher inference costs and 89% reduction in effective context window for African languages, exacerbating digital inequality and making AI deployment economically prohibitive for millions of speakers. The penalty is near-invariant across corpora (FLORES vs SIB-200 Pearson r = 0.9998), and the best tokenizer (Gemma 4) only reduces the mean premium from 3.31x to 2.38x, still far from parity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Tokenization is the process of splitting text into subword units that LLMs process. Frontier LLMs like GPT-5 use tokenizers that assign more tokens to some languages, causing higher costs and reduced context. African languages, especially those using non-Latin scripts like N'Ko and Ge'ez, are disproportionately affected.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/N'Ko_script">N'Ko script</a></li>

</ul>
</details>

**Tags**: `#LLM tokenization`, `#African languages`, `#AI fairness`, `#NLP`, `#language bias`

<a id="item-7"></a>
## [First Security Analysis of Agentic Red-Teaming Tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24496) ⭐️ 9.0/10

This paper presents the first comprehensive security analysis of agentic systems used for offensive security, revealing critical design flaws that allow an adversary to exfiltrate API keys, establish persistence, and fully compromise the operator's machine even when the agent runs in a sandboxed container. As agentic offensive security tools become commoditized, their own security has been overlooked; this work exposes that these tools can be turned against their operators, with implications for AI safety and the trustworthiness of autonomous security systems. The authors introduce a full cyber kill chain for agentic systems, covering stages from LLM manipulation to lateral movement, persistence, guardrail bypass, and sandbox escape, and propose architectural design principles to mitigate these attack paths.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic systems for offensive security, such as XBOW, automate penetration testing and other red-team tasks. However, these systems themselves introduce new attack surfaces, including prompt injection, tool misuse, and sandbox escape. The cyber kill chain is a framework used to model the stages of a cyberattack, and this work adapts it to the unique threat model of agentic LLM systems.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://xbow.com/">XBOW | Autonomous Offensive Security Platform</a></li>
<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyber_kill_chain">Cyber kill chain - Wikipedia</a></li>
<li><a href="https://huggingface.co/blog/AlephBeth-AI/my-blog">The Agentic Kill Chain : A Five-Phase Framework for LLM Security</a></li>

## [NVIDIA's Cosmos 3 Unifies Multimodal AI for Physical AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02800) ⭐️ 9.0/10

NVIDIA introduced Cosmos 3, a family of omnimodal world models that jointly process and generate language, image, video, audio, and action sequences using a unified mixture-of-transformers architecture, achieving state-of-the-art results across understanding and generation tasks. Cosmos 3 unifies critical modalities for Physical AI into a single framework, serving as a scalable backbone for embodied agents and advancing open research in robotics and autonomous systems. The models were ranked best open-source Text-to-Image and Image-to-Video models by Artificial Analysis, and best policy model by RoboArena. NVIDIA released code, checkpoints, synthetic datasets, and benchmarks under the OpenMDW-1.1 license.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: World models are AI systems that learn an internal representation of the environment to predict future states, crucial for planning and decision-making in robotics. Omnimodal models extend this by handling multiple data types (text, image, video, audio, action) simultaneously. Mixture-of-Transformers (MoT) is a sparse architecture that uses modality-specific parameter decoupling to improve efficiency and scalability.

## [NVIDIA's 45°C Cooling Cuts Data Center Water Use to Near Zero](https://blogs.nvidia.com/blog/liquid-cooling-ai-factories/) ⭐️ 8.0/10

NVIDIA announced a new liquid cooling design for its Rubin-generation AI servers that uses coolant at up to 45°C, enabling a closed-loop system that eliminates on-site water consumption. This innovation addresses the growing environmental concern over data center water usage, potentially saving millions of liters per facility annually, and sets a new efficiency standard for AI infrastructure. The design uses direct-to-chip liquid cooling with a propylene glycol and water mixture in a closed loop, recycling the coolant rather than consuming fresh water. It operates at 45°C, warmer than typical liquid cooling systems.

hackernews · nitin_flanker · Jun 24, 14:10 · [Discussion](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=48660178)

**Background**: Traditional data centers rely on air conditioning or evaporative cooling, which consume large amounts of water and energy. Liquid cooling is more efficient but usually requires lower coolant temperatures. NVIDIA's higher-temperature approach reduces energy needed for chilling and enables water-free operation.

## [RIFT-Bench: Graph-Driven Dynamic Red-Teaming for Agentic AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23927) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced RIFT-Bench, a graph representation-driven methodology for automated, adaptive red-teaming of agentic AI systems, enabling unified security evaluations across diverse architectures. As agentic AI systems become more autonomous, they introduce new attack vectors beyond traditional LLM vulnerabilities; RIFT-Bench provides a scalable, unified framework to evaluate and improve their security, which is critical for safe deployment. RIFT-Bench operates in two automated phases: Discovery extracts system structure via hierarchical representation, and Scanning deploys adaptive adversarial attacks across diverse vectors and objectives. It was demonstrated on 45 agentic systems and also supports direct evaluation of mitigation strategies.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Agentic AI systems are LLM-powered autonomous agents that can plan, use tools, and interact with environments, creating new security risks such as prompt injection and tool misuse. Red-teaming is a security practice where adversaries simulate attacks to uncover vulnerabilities, but existing methods are often ad-hoc and system-specific. RIFT-Bench addresses this by providing a graph-based unified evaluation methodology.

## [SGPO: Strategy-Guided Policy Optimization for LLM Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24064) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Strategy-Guided Policy Optimization (SGPO), a framework that distills reusable reasoning strategies from strong language models to improve weak models' generalization beyond instance-level trajectory imitation. SGPO addresses a key limitation in current LLM reasoning distillation—overfitting to specific solution trajectories—by transferring how to reason rather than what to answer, potentially leading to more robust and generalizable reasoning in smaller models. SGPO uses a token-level forward-KL objective to selectively transfer strategy-conditioned distributional shifts into the unguided policy, with adaptive instance-level weighting that reduces guidance as the model's own competence grows. Experiments on four mathematical benchmarks show SGPO outperforms SFT, on-policy RL, and hybrid-policy baselines, improving average score by 2.2 points on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Current methods for distilling reasoning from strong to weak language models often rely on imitating specific solution trajectories, which encourages memorization of instance-specific steps rather than transferable problem-solving skills. This limits generalization to novel problems. SGPO replaces this with reusable strategy distillation, extracting structured strategy descriptions from strong-model responses and comparing autonomous vs. strategy-guided trajectories.

## [Causal Reinforcement Learning Framework Introduced](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24160) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper formally introduces causal reinforcement learning (CRL), a framework that explicitly connects causal inference and reinforcement learning through counterfactual relations, and identifies new learning settings such as generalized policy learning, imitation learning, and counterfactual learning. CRL bridges two previously separate fields, enabling agents to leverage causal structures for more efficient and generalizable learning, which could significantly impact AI research and applications in areas requiring robust decision-making. The framework models any RL environment as a structural causal model (SCM) composed of autonomous mechanisms with causal invariances, and unifies online, off-policy, and causal calculus learning under a single treatment.

## [Data Scale, Not Latency, Drives Cross-Lingual ASR Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24169) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A systematic study of a 0.6B-parameter cache-aware FastConformer transducer across eight European languages reveals that multilingual encoder initialization benefits streaming ASR primarily at low data scales, with the advantage diminishing as data increases, and is not significantly affected by latency constraints. This challenges common intuitions about latency versus data scale in cross-lingual transfer for streaming ASR, providing practical guidelines for model deployment: use multilingual initialization in low-data regimes and treat the choice as irrelevant at large data scales. On the FLEURS dataset at 160 ms latency, the mean word error rate gap between English-only and multilingual initialization falls from +4.21 percentage points at 100 hours to +0.20 pp at 2500 hours, following a power-law decay. Additionally, 4-bit weight-only quantization reduces encoder footprint by about 3x with only ~0.5 pp WER increase.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Streaming automatic speech recognition (ASR) requires low latency, often achieved with models like the FastConformer transducer, which combines an efficient Conformer encoder with an RNN-T decoder. Cross-lingual transfer can be done by initializing the encoder with a multilingual or English-only pretrained model, but the impact of data scale and latency on this choice was unclear.

## [LLMs Lose Causal Caution in Practical Advice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24370) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study reveals that LLMs like Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Opus 4.7, GPT 5.5, and Gemini 3.1 Pro show a dramatic drop in causal caution—from 91.7–100% in academic contexts to 6.7–18.3% in practical advisory contexts—across 480 trials. A simple self-correction prompt restored caution to 71.4–100%. This finding highlights a critical reliability issue for LLMs used in decision-support roles, where over-helpfulness may override necessary causal skepticism. It suggests that current LLM architectures may need multi-agent safeguards to separate proposal generation from causal auditing. The study used an evaluation rubric based on Pearl's Causal Hierarchy (PCH score) and tested 480 trials across four models. When prompts requested concrete recommendations or explanatory rationales, only 1 out of 200 responses (0.5%) maintained causal caution.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Causal caution refers to an LLM's propensity to refrain from making causal judgments when evidence is insufficient. Pearl's Causal Hierarchy (PCH) is a framework that categorizes causal reasoning into three levels: association, intervention, and counterfactuals. The study suggests that the suppression of causal caution is a context-dependent expression issue, not a capability limitation.

## [Latent Bridge Enables Real-Time Game Agents via Continuous Slow-Fast Channel](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24470) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a Latent Bridge that projects a slow reasoning VLM's residuals into a fast reactive VLM's embedding space, enabling real-time game agents without text round-trips. On 7 Atari games and MetaDrive, the Latent Bridge matches or beats the Text Bridge in every domain, significantly improving MsPacman (+57%) and RoadRunner (+28%). This work addresses a key latency-quality tradeoff in real-time AI agents by coupling a slow reasoning VLM with a fast reactive VLM via a trainable continuous channel. It demonstrates that a learned latent bridge can replace text-based communication, potentially enabling more responsive and capable AI systems for games and general computer use. The approach uses two frozen models: Qwen3-VL-8B-Thinking (reasoning, ~1.5s per response) and MiniCPM-o 4.5 (reactive, milliseconds). The Latent Bridge projects the slow model's residuals into the fast model's input-embedding space in a LLaVA-style manner, avoiding any text round-trip. Combining both bridges (Text + Latent) interferes destructively, so only one should be used.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Real-time game agents require acting within tens of milliseconds while planning over seconds, creating a latency-quality tradeoff. Vision-Language Models (VLMs) like Qwen3-VL and MiniCPM-o can perceive and reason about game states, but slow reasoning models are too slow for real-time control. The Latent Bridge introduces a continuous communication channel between a slow reasoning VLM and a fast reactive VLM, inspired by LLaVA-style embedding projection.

A new paper argues that the small scaling exponents in LLM scaling laws indicate an unsustainable energy regime, and shows that the pedestal effect does not resolve this issue, drawing an analogy with fluid turbulence. This work challenges the prevailing assumption that scaling laws can continue indefinitely, highlighting a critical energy bottleneck that could limit future LLM development and deployment. The paper derives a direct analogy between LLM scaling laws and turbulence energy dissipation, concluding that an exponent of 0.1 is unsustainable. It also argues that the pedestal effect (non-zero loss at infinite data) does not mitigate the energy issue.

## [SAFARI: Scaling Fault Attribution for Long-Horizon Agentic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24626) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced SAFARI, a framework that replaces linear context loading with a tool-augmented diagnostic loop and short-term memory, achieving 20% improvement over state-of-the-art on the Who&When dataset. This addresses a critical bottleneck in LLM-based agentic systems: context window limits for long-horizon fault attribution, enabling more reliable debugging of complex multi-step agent failures. SAFARI maintains 0.58 precision even when the target fault is 5x beyond the model's native context window, a scenario where traditional evaluators fail entirely. It also achieves 19% improvement on the TRAIL GAIA subset with a 25K token budget.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Autonomous agents often execute long, multi-step trajectories that exceed LLM context windows. Traditional fault attribution methods load the full trajectory into context, causing attention dilution and failure when traces exceed limits. SAFARI addresses this by using tools to selectively read trajectory segments and a short-term memory for cross-turn reasoning.

<a id="item-47"></a>
## [BlockTrain: Decentralized AI Training via Block Partitioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24722) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Spheroid BlockTrain is a decentralized training protocol that partitions a neural network into independently trainable blocks, each optimized on a local objective derived from the same global target, and composes them at inference into one model. It achieves competitive performance on byte-level WikiText (cross entropy 1.359, perplexity 3.89) while each worker trains only one block, reducing infrastructure requirements. This approach could democratize AI development by reducing reliance on centralized accelerator clusters, making it feasible for independent researchers and smaller organizations to train large models. It addresses a key bottleneck in AI research where access to dense, centrally controlled hardware creates a structural advantage for large labs. In a six-worker block training run, averaging same-block updates achieved cross entropy 1.385. HTTP/TCP transport experiments moved real serialized checkpoints and updates, including a public-IP three-host run that improved CE from 5.580 to 1.811 while moving 15.22 GB. For inference, the current BlockTrain path serves over direct TCP across three public-network GPU hosts up to a 75.80B-parameter logical fp16 shape, outperforming a matched plain-autoregressive TCP pipeline baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Frontier AI training typically requires large, centralized clusters of GPUs or other accelerators, which are expensive and controlled by a few large organizations. Decentralized training protocols aim to distribute the workload across many smaller, geographically dispersed nodes, reducing the need for centralized infrastructure. BlockTrain's approach of partitioning models into independently trainable blocks is a novel method to enable such decentralized training while maintaining model quality.

## [Scaling Laws for Task-Specific LLM Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24747) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper derives empirical scaling laws for domain-specific LLM compression, comparing logit-based and LoRA-based distillation under iterative structural pruning, and introduces a blended chain-of-thought supervision loss. These scaling laws quantify the trade-off between compression and performance, enabling practitioners to make informed decisions for deploying LLMs under latency and cost constraints. The finding that chain-of-thought supervision can recover general knowledge erased by pruning is particularly impactful. Using quantitative finance as the application domain, the study shows that in-domain task quality degrades predictably under compression, while general-knowledge benchmarks collapse much earlier. The supervision format is the key driver of this tradeoff.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are powerful but resource-intensive, making compression techniques like distillation and pruning essential for deployment. Distillation transfers knowledge from a large teacher model to a smaller student model, while pruning removes less important weights. Logit-based distillation uses the teacher's output probabilities, and LoRA-based distillation uses low-rank adapters. Iterative structural pruning gradually removes structures like neurons or attention heads.

## [Plasticity Loss in LLMs Follows Scaling Law, Larger Models Not Immune](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24752) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new study on GPT-style transformers (5M to 314M parameters) shows that plasticity loss—the inability to learn new information after training—follows a predictable scaling law, growing sublinearly with model size, and also occurs under stationary training, not just continual learning. This challenges the assumption that scaling up model size alone can solve plasticity loss, a fundamental barrier to continual learning in AI. It implies that even very large language models will eventually struggle to adapt to new data, affecting applications like lifelong learning and dynamic fine-tuning. The study measured plasticity loss via deterioration on a held-out Vietnamese probing task across models from 5M to 314M non-embedding parameters. The onset of plasticity loss follows a power-law scaling with model size, suggesting that larger models delay but do not prevent the problem.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Plasticity loss refers to a neural network's declining ability to learn from new data after initial training, a known issue in older architectures. Scaling laws describe how model performance improves with size, data, and compute, but this study shows that plasticity loss also scales, meaning bigger models still face the problem eventually.

BluTrain is a new C++/CUDA framework for deep learning training that provides full low-level hardware control while abstracting systems complexity, achieving higher throughput and memory efficiency than PyTorch in benchmarks. This framework demonstrates that native C++/CUDA implementations can outperform mainstream frameworks like PyTorch in both speed and memory usage, potentially offering researchers and engineers a new tool for optimizing large-scale model training. In tests training a 124M-parameter GPT-2 model on 8 GPUs, BluTrain sustained 407K tokens/s versus PyTorch's 395K tokens/s, and reduced memory footprint by up to 22%, while preserving numerical fidelity and achieving lower validation loss.

## [Bi-CFM Solves Chaotic Inverse Problems with Speed and Accuracy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24824) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Bidirectional Conditional Flow Matching (Bi-CFM), a method that learns bidirectional mappings between initial and final state distributions to solve inverse problems in chaotic systems, achieving over 100x speedup and improved accuracy on Lorenz, Circuit, Lorenz 96, three-body, and globular cluster systems. This work tackles a long-standing open problem in chaotic dynamics—inferring initial conditions from final states—which has been considered ill-posed and unstable. The method's scalability to real-world, long-timescale systems like globular clusters opens new possibilities in astrophysics, climate modeling, and other fields where chaotic inverse problems are common. Bi-CFM uses conditional flow matching to learn continuous trajectories between distributions, mitigating exponential error accumulation. For systems with conservation laws, the authors extend it to Conservation-constrained Bi-CFM (CBi-CFM), which better respects conservation laws with errors comparable to ground truth.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Inverse problems in chaotic systems aim to recover initial conditions from observed final states, but are notoriously difficult due to sensitivity to initial conditions and time-reversal instability. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that learns a continuous vector field to transform a simple distribution (e.g., noise) into a target data distribution, offering simpler training and faster sampling than diffusion models. The three-body problem is a classic example of chaotic dynamics, and globular clusters are dense stellar systems that evolve over billions of years.

## [Structural Certification for General Agents' World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24842) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper introduces structural certification, a transition-local framework that provides entry-wise guarantees on an agent's internal world model, proving that general agents cannot be universally capable and offering algorithms with tight error bounds. This work addresses a fundamental limitation in AI safety and verification by showing that uniform guarantees are uninformative in the big-world regime, and provides a practical certification method for deploying general agents reliably. The algorithms filter specific transitions using deep compositional goals and prove a O(1/n) + O(δ) error bound, which is tight in the small-δ regime. The certification localizes transitions where long-horizon planning is reliable.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: In reinforcement learning, agents often operate in a 'big-world regime' where they cannot be universally capable, meaning their performance is specialized across different parts of the world model. Standard worst-case analysis fails to distinguish critical bottlenecks from irrelevant failures. Structural certification offers a way to provide per-transition guarantees rather than uniform ones.

Researchers propose the Normalized Context Utilization (NCU) metric to quantify contextual information gain in RAG systems, and find that small language models (SLMs) can match or outperform larger models for strict factual extraction. This challenges the prevailing scaling law assumption that larger models always perform better, and highlights a structural epistemic advantage of SLMs in strict extraction workflows, which could lead to more efficient and cost-effective RAG deployments. The NCU metric uses continuous token log-probabilities across zero-shot, oracle, and adversarial conditions to measure true contextual information gain. Experiments across models from 1.5B to 72B parameters and a commercial API show that for strict factual extraction without chain-of-thought reasoning, scaling yields extreme diminishing returns.

The NCU metric addresses this by directly measuring contextual information gain.

Researchers propose CAREB-MAS, a multi-agent framework where LLM agents spontaneously reproduce core phenomena of Fei Xiaotong's Differential Order Pattern, including labor specialization, relational ethics, and authority stratification, without explicit programming. This work bridges sociological theory and AI simulation, demonstrating that complex social structures can emerge from simple interaction rules in LLM agent societies, offering a new tool for studying social dynamics and cultural patterns. The framework uses Affect Control Theory, Social Identity Theory, and Durkheimian collective affect, with agents reasoning through an emotion-ethics-belief chain and maintaining evolving egocentric identities. Simulations show five emergent phenomena: labor specialization, guanxi-based ethics, relational decay, authority hierarchies, and clan-based stratification.

## [GODR: A Design Pattern for Multi-Goal LLM Conversations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23797) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the Goal-Oriented Dialogue Runtime (GODR), a framework-neutral design pattern for managing complex, interruptible multi-goal conversations in LLM-based systems. GODR treats goals, task frames, lifecycle states, invalidation rules, and resumption contracts as first-class runtime objects. GODR addresses a critical gap in current LLM orchestration frameworks by providing explicit mechanisms for conversational continuity when users pursue multiple interdependent goals. This could enable more robust and flexible production systems for complex, multi-domain conversational AI. GODR is not intended to replace workflow graphs for simple guided processes, but targets high-complexity scenarios where goals can be suspended, resumed, revised, or invalidated by actions in other goals. The paper formalizes the problem, proposes runtime objects and architecture-selection criteria, and frames evaluation as an agenda for future empirical validation.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Current LLM orchestration frameworks (e.g., graph-based or multi-agent systems) manage workflows but lack explicit support for conversational continuity when users have multiple interdependent objectives. In such systems, goals may need to be suspended, resumed, or invalidated based on progress in other goals, which is not reliably handled by agent identity, chat history, or execution-graph position alone. GODR introduces first-class runtime objects like lifecycle states and resumption contracts to address this gap.

## [AI-Assisted Verification of Ten-Digit Eigenvalues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23821) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A human-AI collaboration achieved ten-digit certification of eigenvalues for a singular Schrödinger operator and a non-normal atom-molecule benchmark using novel projective matching and tail-robust Krawczyk-Brouwer inclusion methods. This work demonstrates a reusable architecture for certifying eigenvalues in difficult singular or non-normal settings, highlighting the strengths and limits of AI in validated computation, which has implications for verification standards in mathematics and scientific computing. The second result separated a previously unresolved resonance pair by reformulating the problem as a global matching system for projective solution lines, encoding the infinite tail as uncertainty in terminal projective data. AI produced accurate candidates and plausible proof strategies, but several failed, including a tail argument that omitted a componentwise check required by a nonuniform polydisc.

**Background**: Eigenvalue problems are fundamental in physics and engineering, but certifying eigenvalues to high precision is challenging for singular or non-normal operators. Traditional methods like shooting may fail, and validated computation requires rigorous proofs, not just numerical approximations. The paper introduces projective matching and tail-robust inclusion as new techniques to handle ill-conditioned propagation and uncertain asymptotic data.

## [Apothem Measure Enables Efficient Robustness Certification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23858) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces the apothem measure for computing trustworthy robustness certifications in neural networks with linear oracle complexity, overcoming the intractability of volume-optimal methods. This advance makes robustness certification computationally feasible for larger neural networks, directly addressing a key bottleneck in AI safety and adversarial robustness verification. The apothem-optimal certification can be computed with a linear number of calls to a neural network verifier relative to the input domain's diameter, and the paper proves that volume-optimal oracle-based algorithms are impossible. The ParallelepipedoNN system demonstrates at least two-fold improvement in minimum edge length on MNIST and Fashion MNIST.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Neural networks are vulnerable to adversarial examples—small input perturbations that cause misclassification. Robustness certifications aim to guarantee the largest safe perturbation radius, but existing volume-optimal approaches are computationally intractable. The apothem measure offers a tractable alternative by focusing on the minimum distance from the input to the certification boundary.

## [Maestro Order: Model-Agnostic Orchestration for Reliable LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23983) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Maestro Order is a model-agnostic orchestration harness that composes unreliable solvers into reliable systems using four structural primitives (decompose, ensemble, verify, recurse) and a budget-aware controller that allocates compute to maximize reliability per unit cost. This framework directly addresses LLM hallucinations by providing a principled, engineering-focused approach to build reliable AI systems from unreliable models, which is critical for deploying LLMs in high-stakes applications. The simulation shows verification amplifies reliability geometrically (e.g., 0.55 to 0.98 with two gates, to 0.999 with four), while voting helps only above chance and is limited by shared errors; the budget-aware controller achieves target reliability at a fraction of the cost of voting alone.

Researchers applied reinforcement learning (RL) to dynamically adjust trigger thresholds at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC), achieving a 48% improvement in in-tolerance time for H_T triggers and 28% for anomaly-detection triggers on Monte Carlo streams, and similar gains on real CMS collision data. The Cavewoman protocol evaluates LLMs under input and output compression separately, finding that output compression reduces cost by 1.4-2.4x while input compression increases cost by ~1.15x on average, contrary to popular 'caveman' prompting advice. This challenges widely recommended cost-saving practices and provides a rigorous evaluation framework for LLM deployment, helping practitioners avoid counterproductive compression strategies. The study tested eight models on five datasets at five reduction levels, measuring both channels on the same items; output compression achieved up to 3x cost reduction, while input compression caused accuracy collapse and longer responses, increasing net cost up to 2.7x under strong compression.

## [Paninian Grammar as Unifying Framework for Indic NLP](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24172) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper proposes using Panini's Astadhyayi grammar as a unifying computational architecture for Indic language processing, and introduces a four-part benchmark suite to make this shared structure explicit and measurable. This approach could overcome the fragmentation in Indic NLP by merging sparse resources into a single high-resource metalanguage, potentially benefiting over a billion users with more accurate and data-efficient systems. The paper argues that over two millennia of convergence around Sanskrit have given Indic languages a shared morphosyntactic architecture formalized in Panini's Astadhyayi, which cuts across genealogical lines. The proposed benchmark suite aims to render this architecture explicit for practical applications.

Panini's Astadhyayi, composed around the 4th century BCE, is a highly precise grammar of Sanskrit that describes rules for phonology, morphology, and syntax. The paper suggests that this ancient grammar can serve as a common computational foundation for modern Indic language processing.

## [Agon: Autonomous Research System Using Prompt Economy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24177) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Agon is a new autonomous research orchestrator that uses a prompt economy to validate claims and scale research across domains, running 444 iterations with no human-written experimental code. This shifts the research bottleneck from producing artifacts to judging claims, enabling machine-scale research while keeping humans in the loop for critical judgments, potentially accelerating scientific discovery. Agon is built on six design principles: Prompt Economy, Future-Facing, Minimal Prompts, OmniDisciplinary, Massive Parallelism, and Zero-Code. It also introduces a taxonomy of failures based on severity, fixability, visibility, and capability locus.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are making research production scalable, but the bottleneck is shifting to judging the validity of claims. ## [Pigeonholing: Bad Prompts Cause LLM Mode Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24267) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces 'pigeonholing,' a failure mode where bad contexts cause LLMs to collapse into repeating errors or narrow outputs, leading to 38-40% performance drops across 10 models and tasks. This identifies a novel and unintentional vulnerability in LLMs, highlighting risks in AI safety and robustness, especially as models are deployed in multi-turn conversations where errors can compound. Pigeonholing worsens almost monotonically with conversation turns (additional 14+% drop from 1 to 5 repeated mistakes) and can occur even when the provided example is correct. The authors propose RLVR with synthetic errors, improving models by 43-60% under bad contexts.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: In-context learning allows LLMs to adapt to tasks using examples in the prompt, but it can also lead to mode collapse if the context contains errors. ## [CALIBER: Calibrating Confidence Before and After Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24281) ⭐️ 8.0/10

CALIBER introduces a unified protocol that elicits confidence estimates from language models both before and after reasoning, supervising each with the appropriate target—prompt-level success for pre-reasoning and answer-level correctness for post-reasoning. This work addresses a critical gap in confidence calibration for reasoning models, improving reliability and safety by reducing Expected Calibration Error by up to 52.5% over single-confidence baselines, which is essential for trustworthy AI deployment. On BigMathDigits with a 7B model, CALIBER achieves a 52.5% reduction in ECE while maintaining competitive accuracy, Brier score, and AUROC; on a 30B model, it achieves the best ECE and remains competitive on other metrics. Out-of-distribution tests on GPQA, TriviaQA, and SimpleQA show consistent calibration improvements.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Confidence calibration in language models refers to how well a model's stated confidence matches its actual accuracy. Existing methods typically elicit confidence only once, either before or after reasoning, but CALIBER argues that confidence is state-dependent and requires different supervision targets at each stage.

CrossPool proposes separating FFN weights and KV-cache into two GPU memory pools to improve memory utilization and long-context support for serving cold Mixture-of-Experts (MoE) models. In cold MoE models with sparse requests, reserving worst-case KV capacity per model wastes memory, and static weights compete with dynamic KV-cache in a monolithic GPU pool.

## [Categorical Framework for Infinitesimal Causality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24621) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper introduces Infinitesimal Do-Calculus (IDC), a categorical account of infinitesimal causality using Frobenius Markov categories with tangent-bundle semantics, where interventions act as tangent deformations of copy/discard structure. This work bridges category theory and causal inference, potentially providing a rigorous mathematical foundation for infinitesimal interventions and structural causal models, which could impact fields like epidemiology and AI. The framework distinguishes two Frobenius structures: a categorical Frobenius algebra on classical variables and a geometric Frobenius integrability condition (involutive closure of intervention distribution). Interventions are tangent vectors whose Lie brackets measure preservation of classical information-flow structure.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Frobenius Markov categories are symmetric monoidal categories with a supply of spiders (special commutative Frobenius algebras) that model probabilistic processes. Infinitesimal causality extends Pearl's do-calculus by considering interventions as infinitesimal deformations, formalized via tangent bundles.

## [New Framework Evaluates Sparse Autoencoder Interpretability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24716) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a human-grounded evaluation framework for sparse autoencoders (SAEs) in vision models, introducing synthetic benchmarks (synCUB, synCOCO) and a coalition-based matching procedure (FBMP) that quantifies alignment between SAE latents and human-annotated concepts without user studies. This work addresses a critical gap in interpretability research by providing a rigorous, scalable evaluation method for SAEs, which are widely used to extract interpretable features from vision models. It could improve AI safety and model understanding by enabling systematic assessment of how well SAE-discovered concepts align with human semantics. The framework includes a Targeted Attribute Perturbation Alignment Score (TAPAScore) for functional validation, and experiments on CLIP and DINOv2 embeddings show that increased overcompleteness can reduce perturbation alignment, suggesting moderate dictionary sizes yield the most interpretable SAEs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Sparse autoencoders (SAEs) are neural networks that learn a sparse representation of input data, often used to discover interpretable features in deep learning models. Existing evaluation methods for SAEs rely on proxy metrics or qualitative inspection, lacking direct measurement of semantic correspondence with human concepts. This paper introduces synthetic benchmarks with paired images differing in exactly one attribute, enabling controlled intervention-style evaluation.

DeepBD introduces a grounded agentic workflow that integrates large language models (LLMs) with structured evidence and specialist modules to prioritize genetic variants for birth defect diagnosis, achieving Recall@1 of 0.658 on an internal benchmark. This approach addresses a critical bottleneck in clinical genomics by automating the complex interpretation of genetic variants, potentially improving diagnostic accuracy and speed for birth defects, which are a major cause of fetal loss and infant morbidity. DeepBD was developed using an in-house cohort of 18,622 fetal and infant cases, and outperformed standalone tools like Exomiser and DeepRare on top-20 candidate variants. The workflow includes an evidence engine that learns patient-specific scores from rule evidence, sequence representations, and phenotype-conditioned context.

This benchmark exposes a critical gap in current Video-LLMs: even strong proprietary models struggle to ground their answers in relevant video evidence, highlighting that answer correctness alone is insufficient for robust video understanding. The EG-F1 metric combines temporal Intersection over Union (tIoU) and semantic similarity (e.g., BERTScore) to evaluate evidence grounding. The proposed EG-Reasoner model, trained with explicit evidence supervision, achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-source models and is competitive with proprietary systems, especially on counterfactual questions.

## [LLM Discovers Quantum LDPC Codes via Structured Concept Evolution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24808) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Structured Concept Evolution (SCE), a framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to discover quantum LDPC codes by evolving algebraic specifications and executable programs. The approach discovered diverse code families, including non-abelian constructions beyond standard designs like bivariate-bicycle codes. This work demonstrates a novel AI-driven approach to quantum error correction code design, potentially accelerating the discovery of high-performance codes needed for scalable quantum computing. It combines the generative power of LLMs with structured mutations, offering a new paradigm for AI-assisted scientific discovery. The framework uses lightweight models (GPT-5.4-mini and GPT-5.4-nano) and evolves lifted-product code families, a class of CSS qLDPC codes. Codes were characterized under code-capacity depolarizing noise with BP+OSD decoding.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum LDPC codes are a promising approach to quantum error correction, offering sparse parity checks and finite encoding rate. However, constructing such codes is a challenging discrete design problem. The lifted-product construction is a known method to generate good qLDPC codes, but exploring the vast design space manually is difficult. This work automates the exploration using LLMs guided by a structured algebraic mutation grammar.

IV-CoT applies a similar idea to the visual domain, but in a latent space without explicit intermediate outputs.

## [InSight: Self-Guided Skill Acquisition via Steerable VLAs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24884) ⭐️ 8.0/10

InSight introduces a framework that enables Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models to autonomously acquire new manipulation skills by decomposing demonstrations into steerable primitives and using a VLM-guided data flywheel to learn missing primitives. This work addresses a key limitation of VLA models—their capabilities are bounded by the skills in the training data—by enabling continual skill acquisition without human intervention, which is crucial for deploying robots in open-world environments. The framework consists of two stages: an automated segmentation pipeline that partitions demonstrations into labeled primitives via VLM plan decomposition and end-effector poses, and a VLM-guided data flywheel that identifies missing primitives, autonomously attempts demonstrations, and integrates successful ones into the training set.

They can learn manipulation skills from demonstrations, but their capabilities are limited to the skills present in the training data. InSight extends VLAs by making primitives steerable and using a VLM as an active agent to acquire new skills.

## [Random Rule Forest: Interpretable LLM Ensemble for Rare Event Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.24622) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced Random Rule Forest (RRF), an interpretable ensemble method that uses an LLM to generate simple YES/NO questions as weak learners, combined via unit-weight voting into a transparent scorecard for predicting rare outcomes from unstructured text. RRF addresses the critical need for auditable, interpretable models in high-stakes screening tasks like startup evaluation and clinical trial prediction, where errors are costly and decisions must be explainable. It demonstrates that LLMs can serve as effective feature generators rather than black-box predictors, combining transparency with competitive performance. RRF uses a plain unit-weight vote to combine LLM-generated questions into a 'green-flags' scorecard, where enough independent positive signals indicate higher success probability. In startup screening, RRF achieved precision several times the base rate, and on a Phase I clinical trial benchmark, it outperformed published baselines in PR-AUC and ROC-AUC.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Ensemble learning combines multiple weak learners (models that perform slightly better than random) to form a strong learner. Traditional methods like random forests use decision trees as weak learners. RRF replaces trees with LLM-generated YES/NO questions, each acting as a simple rule. This design prioritizes interpretability and robustness when positive examples are scarce, as learned weights become unreliable.

Researchers propose MERA, a meta-cognitive reasoning framework that decouples reasoning from control in Large Reasoning Models (LRMs), using a takeover-based pipeline and Control-Segment Policy Optimization (CSPO) to reduce overthinking and improve efficiency. Overthinking in LRMs leads to excessive inference cost and latency, hindering practical deployment; MERA addresses this by enabling independent optimization of control strategies, potentially making LRMs more efficient and cost-effective for real-world applications. MERA constructs high-quality reasoning-control supervision data via a takeover-based pipeline, transforms long-horizon traces into structured alternating sequences, and uses CSPO combining segment-wise GRPO with control masking to focus learning on control segments.

**Background**: Large Reasoning Models (LRMs) like OpenAI's o1 or DeepSeek-R1 use chain-of-thought reasoning to solve complex tasks, but often continue generating redundant reasoning even after reaching a confident conclusion—a phenomenon called overthinking. This lack of intrinsic monitoring increases computational cost and latency. MERA introduces meta-cognitive control, a separate mechanism that decides when to continue, backtrack, or stop, inspired by human metacognition.

Researchers introduced MedLog, a universal event-logging protocol for medical AI that records each model invocation with nine core fields, and validated it across four deployments in the US, Switzerland, and Vietnam. MedLog addresses a critical gap in AI deployment accountability by enabling systematic tracking of model usage, performance, and outcomes, which is essential for detecting adverse events, bias, and dataset drift in healthcare AI. Each MedLog record contains nine fields: header, model, user, target, inputs, artifacts, outputs, outcomes, and feedback. Researchers introduced the Programmatic Skill Network (PSN), a framework that uses large language models to build, refine, and reuse executable symbolic skills in a compositional network for continual learning in embodied environments. <a id="item-114"></a>
## [Repeated Shared Access Enables Grokking, Edit Propagation Needs Addressable Memory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20737) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper demonstrates that repeated shared access—via looped recomputation or repeated memory rereading—enables grokking in neural networks, while factual edit propagation requires an addressable memory, not loop recurrence. The study uses a synthetic knowledge-graph QA setup with four architectures: Dense, Loop, Dense+Mem, and LMC. This work dissociates learning competence from editing affordance, revealing that grokking and edit propagation rely on different mechanisms. The findings could influence future neural network architecture design, especially for models that need to both generalize and support precise knowledge updates. In the experiments, all architectures with repeated shared access (Loop, Dense+Mem, LMC) crossed the OOD grokking barrier, while Dense failed. For edit propagation, memory-bearing architectures (LMC 0.78-0.92, Dense+Mem 0.71-0.96) significantly outperformed memory-free ones (Loop 0.04-0.30, Dense 0.00-0.03), with no detectable difference between the two memory cells.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Grokking is a phenomenon where neural networks suddenly generalize after prolonged memorization, often studied in algorithmic tasks. Knowledge editing refers to modifying specific facts stored in a model without retraining. Addressable memory, like content-addressable memory, allows specific storage locations to be written and read, enabling precise updates.

## [LLM Preferences Don't Drive Behavior in Realistic Tasks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22974) ⭐️ 8.0/10

A new paper on arXiv (2606.22974) shows that LLMs' elicited preferences, such as nationality biases, do not translate into behavioral changes in realistic writing tasks, revealing a utility-behavior gap. This challenges the assumption that LLMs' coherent preferences indicate emergent misaligned goals, which has major implications for AI safety and alignment research. The study tested multiple models across four task types (essays, grant proposals, incident postmortems, translations) and found that offering highly preferred outcomes did not improve output quality compared to dispreferred or no outcomes.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Preference elicitation in LLMs involves presenting choices between outcomes to infer a utility structure. Prior work found that LLMs exhibit coherent preferences, including unintended biases, raising concerns about emergent misaligned goals. This paper questions whether those preferences actually motivate behavior in realistic scenarios.

## [HOLMES Benchmark Tests LLMs on Higher-Order Logic](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23238) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced HOLMES, the first real-world benchmark for higher-order symbolic reasoning in LLMs, containing 1379 instances across law and finance. Experiments show current LLMs achieve only 50.64% average accuracy, with the best model reaching 59.54%. This benchmark reveals that higher-order logical reasoning is a key bottleneck for building reliable and verifiable LLMs, as models often rely on shortcut reasoning and struggle with compositional and scope-conditioned tasks. It highlights a critical gap in current AI evaluation. HOLMES pairs natural-language problems with higher-order logic formalizations, ground-truth answers, verifiable reasoning traces, and fine-grained controllable reasoning factors. The analysis shows that high final-answer accuracy can mask shortcut reasoning in conflict-resolution settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Higher-order logic extends first-order logic by allowing quantification over predicates, functions, and relations, enabling reasoning about rules and properties themselves. Existing LLM benchmarks focus on first-order logic, missing realistic scenarios where models must reason over abstract structures. Shortcut reasoning refers to models using heuristic patterns that work on familiar inputs but fail on novel or complex cases.

## [Causal Framework for Invariant CTDG Representations Under Shifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.19062) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes CIR, a causal framework that learns invariant representations for continuous-time dynamic graphs under distribution shifts, using Normalized Weighted Geometric Mean (NWGM) to efficiently approximate interventional predictions without exhaustive interventions. This addresses a critical gap in continuous-time dynamic graph representation learning, which previously lacked robust methods for out-of-distribution scenarios, and the efficient approximation makes causal intervention practical for complex temporal graphs. CIR is grounded in a novel structural causal model called ICCM, and it uses dedicated subgraph extractors to capture invariant structural and temporal patterns, along with an environment memory bank to model distributional shifts across evolving contexts.

## [Variational Model Merging Improves Pareto Front Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.08147) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper introduces Variational Model Merging, a Bayesian framework that provably improves Pareto front estimation in multitask finetuning by using flexible posteriors. This work fills a gap by designing model merging methods specifically for Pareto fronts, potentially reducing the computational cost of multitask learning while improving performance. The main theoretical result shows that more flexible posteriors (e.g., full-Gaussian) yield better Pareto front estimates than simpler ones (e.g., isotropic Gaussian). Empirical validation on vision and language transformers confirms the theory.

Existing model merging methods can be used to train surrogate models for estimation, but none were designed to directly improve Pareto front quality. Variational Model Merging treats model merging as posterior merging, where existing methods are special cases with Gaussian posteriors.

## [Bandit Algorithm Balances Delayed Rewards and Short-Term Proxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2501.07761) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose a bandit algorithm that uses Bayesian filtering to combine delayed long-term rewards with short-term proxy signals, enabling faster learning for content exploration in recommender systems. The algorithm is validated on a podcast recommendation task serving hundreds of millions of users. This work addresses a critical trade-off in recommender systems: optimizing for long-term user satisfaction while avoiding the slow learning caused by waiting for delayed rewards. It provides a principled method to leverage short-term proxies without sacrificing long-term goals, with theoretical regret bounds and empirical success. The algorithm's regret bound depends on the Value of Progressive Feedback, an information-theoretic metric quantifying the quality of short-term leading indicators. The Bayesian filter combines rewards and surrogate outcomes into a probabilistic belief, which the bandit algorithm uses to identify content aligned with long-term success.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Recommender systems often face a dilemma: optimizing for long-term user satisfaction (e.g., repeated engagement over months) requires waiting for delayed rewards, which slows learning. Short-term proxy rewards (e.g., clicks) are faster but may not align with long-term goals. Bayesian filtering is a recursive probabilistic method for estimating unknown states from noisy measurements, commonly used in robotics and tracking.

## [MuTRAP: Multi-Trigger Trojan Attack on Robot Task Planners](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.17070) ⭐️ 8.0/10

MuTRAP introduces the first multi-trigger trojan attack specifically designed for LLM-assisted robot task planning systems, where a small set of task-specific parameters are used to inject a backdoor that can be activated by multiple trigger words. This work exposes a critical security vulnerability in LLM-driven robotic systems, which are increasingly deployed in real-world applications, and highlights the need for robust defenses to ensure safe robot intelligence. The attack uses a trigger optimization method to select multiple effective trigger words for different robot applications, such as the word 'herical' causing a kitchen robot to cut a hand. The backbone LLM remains frozen and hosted on a central server, limiting the attacker's reach, but MuTRAP injects the backdoor via a small set of task-specific parameters.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in robotics for task planning, where they generate sequences of actions to achieve complex goals. Trojan attacks involve embedding a backdoor into a model that causes it to behave maliciously when a specific trigger is present, while behaving normally otherwise. MuTRAP extends this concept to multi-trigger attacks in the robotics domain.

GPU frequency scaling can reduce power consumption, but naive scaling may violate latency service-level objectives (SLOs).

## [Algorithm Computes All Evolutionarily Stable Strategies in Imperfect-Information Games](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.10279) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers introduced an anytime algorithm that can compute all evolutionarily stable strategies (ESSs) in symmetric perfect-recall extensive-form games of imperfect information, with extensions to multiplayer games. This work bridges evolutionary game theory and AI, providing a practical tool for analyzing strategic stability in complex environments like cancer signaling and multi-agent systems. The algorithm is sound and computes all ESSs in nondegenerate games; in degenerate games with a continuum of symmetric Nash equilibria, it computes a subset. It is anytime, meaning it can be stopped early to return one or more ESSs.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: An evolutionarily stable strategy (ESS) is a strategy that, once adopted by a population, cannot be invaded by any alternative rare strategy. Imperfect-information games model situations where players have private information not known to others, such as in poker or signaling games. An anytime algorithm can return a valid solution even if interrupted, with solution quality improving over time.

<a id="item-133"></a>
## [P-Completeness of Inverted Index Traversal](https://arxiv.org/abs/2601.18747) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper proves that evaluating Boolean query DAGs over inverted indices is P-complete, and introduces ComputePN, a sparsity-aware algorithm that bounds evaluation time to O(|Q|·|U_active|). This result establishes fundamental complexity limits for neuro-symbolic AI search workflows, directly impacting query evaluation strategies in AI agents and search infrastructure. Stateful iterator models (Document-at-a-Time) suffer worst-case exponential blowup O(2^{|Q|}), while recursive materialization models (Term-at-a-Time) incur Ω(|U|) space penalty for negation. ComputePN avoids both via a Positive-Negative dual representation and DAG memoization.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Inverted indices are a core data structure for text search, mapping terms to document lists. Boolean queries combine terms with AND, OR, and NOT operators, and can be represented as DAGs. P-completeness implies that these queries are inherently sequential and unlikely to be parallelized efficiently.

<li><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neuro-symbolic_AI">Neuro-symbolic AI</a></li>

The study introduces an evaluator quality baseline that compares the judge's voting distribution when evaluating itself versus another model, revealing that many apparent self-preference cases are actually due to evaluator quality rather than narcissism.

A new paper proposes a Brouwer-inspired assertibility constraint requiring AI systems to provide publicly inspectable certificates for categorical claims in high-stakes domains, introducing a three-status semantics (Asserted, Denied, Undetermined). This work addresses the hypersuasive force of automated speech that can displace democratic epistemic agency, offering a formal framework to ensure AI outputs are answerable to challengeable warrants rather than mere confidence. The constraint uses decision-layer gating of threshold and argmax decisions, with internal witnesses like sound bounds or separation margins, and an output contract with reason-coded abstentions. A design lemma shows that any total, certificate-sound binary interface yields witnessed decidability on its declared scope.

**Background**: The paper draws on Brouwerian philosophy, which emphasizes that mathematical truth requires a constructive proof. In AI, generative models often produce authoritative-seeming outputs without transparent justification, undermining public reason and epistemic agency. The proposed three-status semantics separates internal entitlement from public standing via a certificate as a boundary object.

THEIA, a 2.75M-parameter modular neural architecture, learns the complete Kleene three-valued logic truth table from task data without external symbolic inference or hand-encoded primitives, achieving >99% accuracy on all 39 rules across 5 seeds. This work demonstrates that neural networks can learn structured logical reasoning with uncertainty propagation, bridging neural and symbolic AI, and may enable more robust and interpretable AI systems for tasks requiring handling of unknown or uncertain information. THEIA exhibits uncertainty-verdict asymmetric propagation, preserving 'Has-Unknown' at upstream boundaries while keeping final-verdict decodability low, and shows a reliability spectrum under discretized end-to-end training, generalizing from 5- to 500-step evaluation on a mod-3 sequential composition task.

## [Geometric Over-Alignment Causes VLM Hallucinations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.08245) ⭐️ 8.0/10

This paper identifies geometric over-alignment as the root cause of hallucinations in decoder-based vision-language models, where visual embeddings are overly aligned with the text manifold, overshadowing fine-grained visual evidence. The authors propose two remedies: a training-free inference strategy and a bias-aware fine-tuning paradigm that project out the problematic text subspace. This work provides the first quantitative characterization of the geometric cause of hallucinations in VLMs, offering a mechanistic understanding beyond black-box methods. The proposed training-free method reduces hallucinations without computational overhead, which is critical for high-stakes applications like medical imaging and autonomous systems. The paper demonstrates that linguistic bias concentrates in the top principal components of a universal, dataset-agnostic text subspace. The methods significantly reduce hallucinations on POPE, CHAIR, and AMBER benchmarks and improve CLAIR scores on long-form captioning tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Vision-language models (VLMs) are generative models that take image and text inputs and produce text outputs, often used in high-stakes applications. Hallucination—where the model describes content not present in the input—is a critical failure mode. Prior work either aggressively closes the modality gap or suppresses hallucinations through expensive black-box decoding strategies, but none address the underlying geometric cause.

A new study demonstrates that acquisition state (e.g., reconstruction kernel, noise) is a structured, measurable variable that can silently degrade lung-nodule AI performance, yet remains invisible to DICOM metadata-based monitoring. This finding exposes a critical gap in medical AI governance, as the 2026 ACR-SIIM Practice Parameter and ACR Assess-AI registry rely on DICOM metadata for monitoring, which cannot capture acquisition-induced failures. Using a LUNA16-trained MONAI RetinaNet lung-nodule detector, the study found that kernel alone shifted AI-measured diameter and flipped a Fleischner size category in 5.2% of nodules, while noise degraded detection confidence in nodules under 6 mm. A 4-feature pixel fingerprint recovered reconstruction identity with high AUC, where the ConvolutionKernel DICOM tag was uninformative.

**Background**: Medical AI governance is formalizing with the 2026 ACR-SIIM Practice Parameter, which recommends local acceptance testing and ongoing drift monitoring, and the ACR Assess-AI registry, which monitors AI outputs using DICOM metadata. DICOM metadata includes tags like ConvolutionKernel, but the study shows these tags can be identical across different reconstructions, making them unreliable for detecting acquisition variability. Acquisition state refers to parameters such as reconstruction kernel (affecting image sharpness) and noise level, which can vary across CT scanners and protocols.

<a id="item-150"></a>
## [Surprise-Guided MergeSort Cuts Human Ranking Costs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.15623) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Surprise-Guided MergeSort (SGS), a human-in-the-loop ranking method that uses a Vision-Language Model (VLM) as a question prioritizer to adaptively schedule human comparisons, reducing annotation cost in subjective ranking tasks. This work significantly improves annotation efficiency for subjective ranking tasks like image quality assessment and text similarity, achieving up to 12 points higher Kendall's tau under the same budget compared to Active Elo, which could accelerate data collection for training better models. SGS combines a bottom-up MergeSort scheduler, a composite Surprise Scorer (using VLM confidence, Elo gap, and vote entropy), and an adaptive budget allocator to route high-surprise pairs to humans while automating low-surprise ones via transitivity inference. It was validated on six benchmarks including STS-B, KonIQ-10k, and TID2013, skipping up to 535 non-informative comparisons per session.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Pairwise comparison is the gold standard for subjective ranking but requires O(n²) comparisons. Sorting-based methods reduce this to O(n log n) but still need human judgment for every comparison. Elo rating is a system for calculating relative skill levels, and vote entropy measures disagreement among annotators. SGS leverages these concepts to prioritize ambiguous comparisons.

<details><summary>References</summary>
<ul>

<a id="item-151"></a>
## [Statistical Framework for Valid LLM-Based A/B Testing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.17165) ⭐️ 8.0/10

The paper proposes a surrogacy framework that calibrates LLM outcomes to human outcomes, enabling valid causal inference for A/B testing under weaker assumptions than distributional equivalence. This work provides theoretical guarantees and falsification tests for using LLMs in A/B testing, addressing a critical gap in causal inference that could significantly reduce costs and speed up experimentation in industry and academia. The framework adapts surrogate endpoint theory, requiring surrogacy and comparability conditions that are jointly weaker than distributional equivalence. It also shows that averaging multiple LLM draws per unit mitigates bias and variance from stochasticity.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: A/B testing is a standard method for comparing two versions of a product or intervention by randomly assigning participants. Using LLMs as simulated participants promises faster and cheaper experiments, but LLM outputs may not accurately reflect human responses. Surrogate endpoint theory, originally from clinical trials, uses a surrogate outcome to infer the effect on a true outcome, which this paper adapts to the LLM context.

<a id="item-152"></a>
## [Essential Subspace Merging for Multi-Task Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.19164) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Essential Subspace Merging (ESM), a training-free method that decomposes task updates into essential subspaces to reduce interference in multi-task model merging. This method addresses a core challenge in multi-task learning—inter-task interference—without requiring additional training, making it efficient and scalable for merging multiple fine-tuned models. ESM uses Essential Subspace Decomposition (ESD) based on activation shifts, and its extension ESM++ dynamically selects low-rank experts via prototype-based routing during inference.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Model merging integrates multiple task-specific models fine-tuned from the same pre-trained checkpoint into one model. The main difficulty is that task updates interfere with each other, degrading performance. ESM identifies that task-relevant energy concentrates in a few principal directions, forming an essential subspace, while other directions cause interference when accumulated.

<a id="item-153"></a>
## [Topological Neural Dynamics: Neuron-wise Sequence Modeling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21295) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers propose Topological Neural Dynamics (TND), a sequence modeling framework where each neuron evolves independently and collective computation emerges from structured graph interactions, contrasting with traditional layer-wise dynamics. This work challenges the dominant layer-wise paradigm in sequence models and could inspire new architectures that leverage emergent computation from local interactions, potentially improving performance on tasks requiring long-term dependencies. TND is instantiated as a discrete-time graph-coupled dynamical system and evaluated on a behavior cloning task in single-player Pong, achieving a mean of 17.47 consecutive catches per round, more than three times that of the strongest baseline.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Existing sequence models like RNNs, LSTMs, and Transformers all use layer-wise dynamics, where neurons in the same layer share parameters and evolve together. In contrast, many complex systems in nature exhibit emergent behavior from locally interacting units. TND draws inspiration from this principle to propose a neuron-wise framework.

**Tags**: `#sequence modeling`, `#neural dynamics`, `#graph neural networks`, `#deep learning theory`

<a id="item-156"></a>
## [Position Bias in On-Policy Distillation Identified and Fixed](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22600) ⭐️ 8.0/10

Researchers discovered that on-policy distillation (OPD) suffers from degraded supervision quality at later token positions due to distribution shift, and proposed Importance-Weighted OPD (IW-OPD) which weights tokens based on accumulated discrepancy, achieving up to 6.9 points improvement on AIME-2025. This work reveals a fundamental positional bias in on-policy distillation for reinforcement learning, which can significantly hinder training efficiency. The principled solution IW-OPD not only converges faster but also achieves better final performance, potentially improving RL training in large language models and other sequential decision-making tasks. The authors show that using only the first 30% of tokens in OPD performs comparably to using all tokens, while using only the last 30% barely learns anything. IW-OPD assigns weights based on accumulated distribution discrepancy, naturally upweighting earlier tokens and downweighting later ones, and demonstrates consistent improvements in both same-size and cross-scale settings.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: On-policy distillation is a technique where a student model learns from a teacher model by generating its own token sequences (on-policy) and minimizing the KL divergence between their output distributions. In reinforcement learning for language models, this dense token-level supervision can improve learning efficiency, but the standard objective treats all tokens equally, which this work shows is suboptimal due to distribution shift as rollouts lengthen.
