A new study analyzing over 500,000 ChatGPT conversations reveals that more than one third involve fiction generation, including original stories, roleplay, fanfiction, and erotica, dominated by power users who repeatedly request variations of similar narratives. This finding challenges traditional author-reader dynamics and raises questions about AI's role in storytelling, potentially leading to a 'solipsistic reader-writer' who generates and consumes fiction in a closed loop with a machine. LemonHarness is a new execution framework that constrains state-changing operations within a defined workspace, improving tracking and control for long-horizon LLM agents. It achieves 86.52% accuracy on Terminal-Bench 2.0 with GPT-5.5. Long-horizon LLM agents often struggle with unconstrained state changes that make tracking difficult. The framework introduces a reusable rule knowledge base and a time-aware execution mechanism that exposes elapsed and remaining budget to the model. On Terminal-Bench 2.0, LemonHarness with GPT-5.3-CodeX achieved 84.49% accuracy over 445 trials, and with GPT-5.5 reached 86.52% average accuracy across five jobs.

Researchers introduced DigenRL, a disaggregated reinforcement learning framework for diffusion-based generative LLMs that decouples rollout and training resources, enabling flexible resource allocation and heterogeneous GPU support. It achieves 1.56-2.10x throughput improvements over existing systems like veRL-Omni and GenRL on models such as HunyuanVideo-13B and FLUX.1-12B. By enabling independent scaling and heterogeneous deployment, DigenRL can accelerate research and deployment of RL fine-tuning for large diffusion models. DigenRL introduces three novel techniques: generation-axis pipeline (GAP) and time-step parallelism (TSP) for finer-grained pipelining, elastic trainer-assisted generation (TAG) for dynamic GPU resource sharing, and a tightly one-step constrained asynchronous strategy to reduce execution bubbles. Experiments were conducted on 16-32 GPUs using four generative models including HunyuanVideo-13B and Wan2.1-14B.

CompressKV introduces a semantic-retrieval-guided KV-cache compression framework for GQA-based LLMs that identifies Semantic Retrieval Heads (SRHs) to selectively retain critical tokens, reducing memory footprint and decoding cost without performance degradation. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in long-context LLM inference by significantly reducing KV-cache memory usage, enabling deployment on resource-constrained hardware while preserving over 97% of full-cache performance with only 3% cache budget. CompressKV leverages the discovery of retrieval heads that specialize in retrieving relevant information from context.

## [LaGO: LLM as Latent Action Prior for Online RL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24669) ⭐️ 7.0/10

LaGO uses a pretrained LLM as a latent action prior to softly guide online policy optimization, rather than as a direct controller, achieving success rate improvements from 15.1% to 27.2% on CLEVR-Robot and from 2.7% to 15.2% on Meta-World. This work demonstrates that LLMs can effectively guide online reinforcement learning without requiring precise action generation, potentially enabling more reliable and sample-efficient RL in complex tasks. This paper presents the first practical exact algorithm for cost-optimal stochastic Boolean function evaluation, using branch-and-bound with variable-selection heuristics, pruning, and caching. The authors also prove the problem is #P-hard and contained in PSPACE. This work bridges a gap between theoretical hardness and practical solvability, enabling optimal decision-making under costly information acquisition. It has direct applications in areas like medical diagnosis, where tests have different costs and probabilities. The algorithm includes a greedy beam-search variant that trades efficiency for quality, and experiments on random instances demonstrate scalability. The paper also evaluates a structured heart-disease diagnosis instance.

The paper compares the novel account to the other six accounts on crucial examples, demonstrating that the distinctions between factual difference-making, counterfactual difference-making, and regularity-based accounts are not meaningful.

## [MGI: New Problem in Generative Model Security](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23872) ⭐️ 7.0/10

A new paper formalizes the Member vs Generated Inference (MGI) problem, showing that existing membership inference and attribution methods fail to distinguish training data from model-generated samples, and proposes Data Circuit Breaker (DCB) to address it. This work highlights a critical blind spot in generative model security, as models increasingly memorize and reproduce training data, making it essential to determine whether a sample is from training or generated. The proposed DCB method could improve accountability and trust in generative AI systems. DCB is a three-stage method that combines complementary signals from a generative model's autoencoder and latent generator. It remains effective even when models reproduce near-duplicates of training samples and generalizes to model derivative settings where new models are trained on generated data.

**Background**: Membership inference attacks aim to determine whether a specific data point was used to train a model, while attribution methods try to identify which training samples influenced a generated output. ## [ARIA: Adaptive Region-Based Importance for Diffusion Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23898) ⭐️ 7.0/10

ARIA is a new framework that adaptively allocates training effort across coarse regions of the conditioning space to improve knowledge distillation in conditional diffusion models. It maintains online estimates of teacher-student discrepancy at the region level and focuses updates where misalignment persists. This addresses a key bottleneck in distilling conditional diffusion models: efficiently exploring a large conditioning space when paired data is limited. By improving distillation efficiency, ARIA could enable faster and more accurate smaller models for text-to-image generation and other conditional tasks. ARIA builds on prior work that switches conditions during training, but adds adaptive importance allocation to decide how much effort to spend on each region. Empirically, ARIA improves over the RC baseline across most architectures and settings, with the clearest gains in unseen and underrepresented regimes.

**Background**: Knowledge distillation in diffusion models involves a smaller student model learning from a larger teacher model. For conditional diffusion models, the student must learn to generate outputs aligned with various conditioning inputs (e.g., text prompts), but the conditioning space can be extremely large, making exhaustive training infeasible.

## [EMAgnet: Adaptive EMA Regularization for Self-Play](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23995) ⭐️ 7.0/10

EMAgnet introduces parameter-space exponential moving average (EMA) regularization for policy gradient self-play, replacing the uniform-magnet regularization used in PPO self-play. It achieves lower exploitability in two-player zero-sum games, especially those with strictly dominated strategies. This work improves the efficiency and robustness of self-play training in large imperfect-information games, which is crucial for AI in poker, strategy games, and other competitive domains. The adaptive regularization target could become a standard component in multi-agent reinforcement learning. EMAgnet regularizes toward an exponential moving average of the policy parameters rather than the uniform distribution, allowing the regularization target to evolve with the agent's improving strategy. It was evaluated on standard benchmarks and modified benchmarks with exploration challenges and large numbers of strictly dominated strategies, outperforming PPO self-play with uniform-magnet regularization under both linear and power-law annealing schedules.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Self-play is a training method where an agent plays against copies of itself to improve, commonly used in games like Go and poker. Policy gradient methods like PPO optimize policies directly, and regularization helps prevent overfitting to current strategies. Uniform-magnet regularization penalizes deviation from a uniform policy, but EMAgnet adapts the target based on recent policy parameters.

## [Certification Token Complexity for Stochastic Oracles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24074) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces certification token complexity, the minimum expected token cost to distinguish reliable stochastic oracles from unreliable ones with controlled error probability, and presents an SPRT-based Turing machine that achieves this with matching upper and lower bounds. This work provides a theoretical foundation for certifying the reliability of stochastic oracles, which is crucial for systems that depend on probabilistic outputs, such as randomized algorithms or machine learning models. The token complexity framework offers a principled way to measure and optimize the cost of reliability certification. The SPRT-based certification SOTM halts almost surely and satisfies two-sided error guarantees, with an explicit upper bound on token complexity in terms of reliability thresholds, error bound, and per-turn token cost. A matching information-theoretic lower bound shows that the SPRT construction is optimal in the leading order as the error bound tends to zero.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: A stochastic oracle is a black-box that returns random outputs based on an unknown distribution. The Stochastic-Oracle Turing Machine (SOTM) framework extends Turing machines with the ability to query such oracles at a token cost. The Sequential Probability Ratio Test (SPRT) is a statistical method that tests hypotheses by sequentially evaluating data, stopping when evidence is sufficient.

DynaWM introduces a dynamics-aware representation learning framework that combines a world model regularizer and momentum target distillation to enable bipedal-wheeled robots to traverse continuous stairs smoothly. This work addresses a key limitation in teacher-student frameworks for legged locomotion—weak dynamics-aware representations and incomplete terrain encoding—potentially advancing the deployment of bipedal-wheeled robots in complex real-world environments like staircases. World models are internal models that predict future states, and momentum targets stabilize training by using slowly updated encoders.

A new study benchmarks nine hallucination detection methods on the Gut-VLM dataset across five vision-language models (VLMs), finding that the white-box method ReXTrust achieves the highest AUC (up to 93.0) and significantly outperforms all other methods. This work addresses a critical safety gap for deploying VLMs in gastrointestinal endoscopy, where hallucinations could lead to misdiagnosis. The results highlight the advantage of white-box methods and identify confident confabulation as a systemic failure mode. ReXTrust, a white-box method using hidden-state access, outperformed the best alternative on each VLM by a statistically significant margin (p < 0.001), with an average advantage of 19.5 AUC points. DTT-BSR+ proposes a two-stage generative-regression cascade for music source restoration, where a generative DTT-BSR separator first fits the distribution of clean sources, and a modified Demucs network then refines the output using time-domain and multi-resolution spectral losses. This approach achieves state-of-the-art results on five stems, outperforming prior systems like X-LANCE MSR, and introduces a novel FAD decomposition that reveals a trade-off between signal reconstruction accuracy and semantic distribution fitting. ## [Zero-Shot Test-Time Canonicalization via OOD Scoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24178) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper reframes test-time canonicalization as out-of-distribution (OOD) detection, allowing any OOD score to be used for finding the best transformation to canonicalize an input image. The authors systematically evaluate about 20 OOD scores and 9 search algorithms, finding that distance-based scores with random search and local refinement work best. This work opens up a new design space for scoring functions in test-time canonicalization, improving vision model robustness against affine transformations without retraining. It also introduces a gated mechanism that preserves in-distribution accuracy by only transforming inputs when needed. The method uses any OOD score as the energy minimized over transformations, and adds a gated mechanism that canonicalizes only when the OOD score indicates the input is out-of-distribution. Experiments span handwritten characters, sketches, natural images, and 3D point clouds.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Affine transformations like rotation, scaling, and shearing can cause pretrained vision models to misclassify images even though the object class remains unchanged. Traditional approaches to robustness require retraining or architectural changes, while test-time canonicalization aims to undo the transformation at inference time without modifying the model. Out-of-distribution (OOD) detection identifies inputs that differ from the training distribution, and this paper repurposes OOD scores to guide the search for the canonical transformation.

The paper identifies the problem of unintended suppression of benign co-occurring concepts during diffusion model unlearning, introduces the CARE score to quantify preservation, and proposes the ReCARE framework to safeguard these concepts while erasing only the target concept. This work addresses a critical limitation in diffusion model unlearning, ensuring that removing harmful content does not inadvertently degrade the model's ability to generate benign related concepts, which is essential for practical AI safety and utility. ReCARE automatically constructs a CARE-set—a curated vocabulary of benign co-occurring tokens extracted from target images—and leverages it during training for stable unlearning. Experiments on nudity, Van Gogh style, and tench object show state-of-the-art performance in balancing erasure, utility, and CARE preservation.

This paper introduces Structural Kolmogorov-Arnold Convolutions that place learnable functions on the filter structure rather than per-edge, proposing three realizations: SV-KAN, AG-KAN, and RF-KAN. ## [FlowPipe: LLM-Enhanced Generative Flow Networks for Data Pipelines](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24679) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FlowPipe introduces a unified framework using Conditional Generative Flow Networks (C-GFlowNets) with a Trajectory Balance objective to construct data preparation pipelines, outperforming existing methods by 11.96% in accuracy and achieving 12.5x faster training convergence. This work addresses key limitations of prior methods like Multi-DQN, such as weak credit assignment and inefficient exploration, by integrating LLM-derived semantic priors and failure awareness, which could significantly automate and improve data preprocessing for machine learning. FlowPipe uses Deep Semantic Modulation via Feature-wise Linear Modulation (FiLM) to condition the policy on dataset semantics, and incorporates failure awareness into the flow objective to avoid invalid states. Experiments were conducted on 74 real-world datasets across two benchmark suites.

Generative Flow Networks (GFlowNets) are a class of generative models that learn to sample objects proportionally to a reward function, and Conditional GFlowNets extend this with conditioning on context. The Trajectory Balance objective improves credit assignment over complete trajectories.

This paper proposes a skip-free encoder-decoder backbone for flow-matching speech enhancement that uses latent representation alignment with a frozen Descript Audio Codec to avoid noise transfer from skip connections. This approach addresses a key limitation of U-Net in generative speech enhancement by preventing noise transfer through skip connections, while maintaining efficient few-step inference, potentially enabling higher quality real-time speech enhancement. The model aligns its bottleneck and decoder representations with clean latent features from a frozen Descript Audio Codec encoder-decoder without quantization. Experiments on WSJ0-CHiME3 and VoiceBank-DEMAND show improved PESQ and perceptual quality using only five function evaluations.

Descript Audio Codec is a neural audio codec that compresses audio into a compact latent space with high fidelity.

## [UniDrive: Unified Framework for Interpretable Risk Understanding in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24759) ⭐️ 7.0/10

UniDrive is a unified vision-language and grounding framework that combines a temporal reasoning branch with a high-resolution perception branch via a gated cross-attention fusion module, enabling joint generation of risk descriptions and bounding-box outputs. It outperforms existing baselines on the DRAMA-Reasoning benchmark, especially in small-object localization and zero-shot generalization. This work addresses a critical trade-off in autonomous driving perception between temporal reasoning and spatial precision, which is essential for safety. By providing interpretable risk understanding with grounded evidence, UniDrive could enhance trust in autonomous driving systems and improve their ability to detect small or occluded hazards. The framework uses a gated cross-attention fusion module to align dynamic context from multi-frame video with precise spatial details from the latest high-resolution frame. Experiments show clear advantages in small-object localization, zero-shot generalization to NuScenes and BDD100K, and human-rated interpretability.

**Background**: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have been applied to autonomous driving scene understanding, but they often struggle with a trade-off: models using single-frame or low-resolution inputs miss small or occluded hazards, while language-centric models lack grounded evidence. UniDrive combines temporal reasoning (from multi-frame video) with high-resolution spatial perception to overcome this limitation. The gated cross-attention fusion mechanism is a technique that integrates information from different modalities using attention and learnable gates.

## [Grad Detect: Gradient-Based Hallucination Detection in LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24790) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Researchers propose Grad Detect, a method that uses layer-wise gradient patterns from a single forward-backward pass during inference to detect hallucinations in large language models (LLMs). It outperforms confidence-based and sampling-based baselines across multiple Q&A benchmarks. Hallucinations in LLMs pose significant risks in high-stakes applications like healthcare and law. Grad Detect offers a unified, interpretable framework for predicting multiple dimensions of LLM reliability, potentially improving trust and safety in LLM deployment. Layer ablation studies across 11 models from 4 architectural families show that the final five layers concentrate over 97% of the discriminative gradient signal, enabling efficient deployment with minimal performance loss. Grad Detect is evaluated on hallucination detection and model abstention prediction tasks.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) sometimes generate plausible but incorrect information, known as hallucinations. Existing detection methods often rely on output confidence scores or multiple sampling passes, which can be unreliable or computationally expensive. Gradients—the derivatives of the loss with respect to model parameters—have been used for model interpretability but not previously for hallucination detection in this manner.

## [OrbitForge: Text-to-3D via Video Priors and Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24799) ⭐️ 7.0/10

OrbitForge introduces a method that uses frozen text-to-video priors and per-prompt Gaussian Splatting optimization to convert a single text-generated video into a consistent 3D scene with a closed orbit. This approach addresses key challenges in text-to-3D generation, such as camera control and view consistency, without requiring task-specific fine-tuning or score-distillation optimization, potentially enabling more accessible and robust 3D content creation. OrbitForge first obtains a preliminary 3D reconstruction via Deformable Gaussian Splatting with a robust MedianGS proxy, then uses the text-to-video model to complete missing views from a prescribed orbit, and finally reconstructs the completed orbit into a final Gaussian Splatting scene.

**Background**: Gaussian Splatting is a volume rendering technique that represents 3D scenes using collections of anisotropic 3D Gaussian primitives, enabling high-quality real-time novel view synthesis. Text-to-video models generate videos from text prompts but often lack 3D consistency, which OrbitForge aims to correct.

TIP-Search introduces a scheduling framework that uses conformal prediction to filter feasible models, dispatches tasks over finite workers, and employs shielded constrained online experts to balance accuracy, queue pressure, and deadline risk, achieving timely accuracy of 0.991 and deadline satisfaction of 0.962 on the TLOB FI-2010 dataset. This work addresses a critical challenge in real-time market prediction: ensuring predictions are both accurate and delivered before deadlines under uncertain load, which is essential for high-frequency trading and other time-sensitive financial applications. The framework includes a conformal latency-quantile filtering step to select feasible models, and uses shielded constrained online experts (OCO-ACPO and SA-OCO-ACPO) to adapt to nonstationary workloads, with statistically significant improvements over baselines like RAMSIS and SneakPeek.

## [SEAL: NAS for Efficient Incremental Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.10457) ⭐️ 7.0/10

SEAL is a new NAS-based framework for incremental learning that dynamically expands model architecture only when necessary, using a capacity estimation metric and cross-distillation training. This addresses the resource constraints of prior NAS-based incremental learning methods that expand at every task, making continual learning more practical for real-world applications with limited memory and compute. SEAL jointly searches for both the architecture and the optimal expansion policy, and uses cross-distillation to preserve stability after expansion. Experiments show it reduces forgetting and improves accuracy while allocating extra capacity only when needed.

**Background**: Incremental learning (continual learning) trains a model on a sequence of tasks without forgetting previous knowledge. Prior NAS-based incremental learning methods expanded the model at every task, which is inefficient in resource-limited settings.

## [Rule2Text: LLM-Generated Explanations for KG Rules](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.10971) ⭐️ 7.0/10

Rule2Text is a framework that uses large language models (LLMs) to generate natural language explanations for logical rules mined from knowledge graphs, improving interpretability. The paper presents extensive experiments with multiple datasets and LLMs, including fine-tuning an open-source model (Zephyr) to achieve significant quality improvements. This work addresses a critical bottleneck in knowledge graph usability: the difficulty humans face in interpreting complex logical rules. By generating clear natural language explanations, Rule2Text makes knowledge graphs more accessible to non-experts and facilitates broader adoption in domains like biomedicine and e-commerce. The framework uses AMIE 3.5.1 for rule mining and evaluates several LLMs (including Gemini 2.0 Flash) with zero-shot, few-shot, variable type, and Chain-of-Thought prompting. It also develops an LLM-as-a-judge evaluation method that aligns well with human judgments, and fine-tunes the Zephyr model on high-quality ground truth data.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Knowledge graphs store structured information as entities and relationships, and rule mining can discover logical patterns (e.g., 'if A is parent of B and B is parent of C, then A is grandparent of C'). However, these rules are often encoded with cryptic labels (e.g., 'r1: p(x,y) ∧ p(y,z) → p(x,z)'), making them hard to interpret. Large language models (LLMs) like GPT-4 and Gemini can generate fluent natural language, offering a way to translate such rules into human-readable explanations.

Knowledge distillation is a technique where a smaller student model is trained to mimic a larger teacher model, often used for model compression.

Researchers propose IRRI (Iterative Refinement of Repair Instructions), a simplified method for multi-turn program correction that fixes initial programs and iteratively refines repair instructions, achieving performance comparable to the complex Scattered Forest Search (SFS) method. This work demonstrates that complex search structures like Monte Carlo Tree Search are not necessary for effective multi-turn program repair, simplifying the approach and making it more accessible. It also provides theoretical grounding via Oracle-Guided Inductive Synthesis (OGIS), ensuring the method's non-elimination property. IRRI is theoretically proven to have the non-elimination property, meaning the correct program is never eliminated from the candidate space and the method eventually converges. Experiments on several program generation benchmarks show IRRI matches state-of-the-art inference performance without complex search.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used for program synthesis, but generating correct code often requires multiple attempts. Scattered Forest Search (SFS) is a state-of-the-art method that uses Monte Carlo Tree Search and carefully crafted initial seeds for multi-turn program correction, but its complexity obscures which components are essential. Oracle-Guided Inductive Synthesis (OGIS) is a framework for iterative synthesis that queries an oracle to guide the search.

## [Ensemble DRO for Robust Bayesian Optimization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.07565) ⭐️ 7.0/10

The paper introduces Ensemble Distributionally Robust Bayesian Optimisation (EDRBO), a novel method that uses ensemble surrogate models and Wasserstein ball ambiguity sets to handle uncertain environmental contexts without discretization or restrictive assumptions. EDRBO addresses key limitations of existing robust BO methods—high computational cost, discretization of continuous contexts, and restrictive assumptions—making it more practical for real-world optimization under uncertainty. EDRBO provides sublinear cumulative regret guarantees of order O(γ_T √T), where γ_T is the maximum information gain within the ensemble, and demonstrates state-of-the-art performance in empirical evaluations.

**Background**: Bayesian Optimization (BO) is a sample-efficient method for optimizing black-box functions, often used in hyperparameter tuning and experimental design. Distributionally Robust Optimization (DRO) aims to make decisions robust to distributional shifts between training and test data. Ensemble surrogate models combine multiple predictive models to improve accuracy and uncertainty quantification.

## [Simplex Witness Certificate and Escape Force for VAE Collapse](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.18224) ⭐️ 7.0/10

This paper introduces a simplex witness certificate and escape force to detect and prevent constant collapse in variational autoencoders, where the encoder mean becomes independent of the input. Constant collapse is a known failure mode in VAEs that undermines their generative performance; this work provides a theoretically grounded method to detect and escape it, potentially improving VAE training reliability. The method uses a fixed teacher posterior from a GMM-based view of the data and a latent-only simplex witness attached to the encoder mean, yielding a certificate and a local escape direction. Experiments on MNIST, CIFAR-10, and CIFAR-100 show that vanilla VAEs fail the certificate while RST variants pass.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Variational autoencoders (VAEs) are generative models that learn a latent representation of data by maximizing a variational lower bound. Constant collapse occurs when the encoder ignores the input and outputs a constant mean, making the latent code uninformative. This paper addresses exact constant collapse of the encoder mean, not other collapse modes.

## [QSignAI: Quantum Randomness for AI Identity Signatures](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.27729) ⭐️ 7.0/10

QSignAI is a production-deployed platform that uses a Toeplitz two-source extractor over independent single-qubit Hadamard measurements on SV1 and DM1 simulators, plus a 2-qubit Bell state, to generate unique quantum-randomness-seeded identity signatures for participants in an AI-driven social system. This work bridges AI and quantum computing in a real-world deployed system, making quantum phenomena perceptually legible to general audiences via an AI bot, and addresses practical latency and legibility challenges. It is timely given the recent Nobel and Turing awards recognizing both fields. The current deployment uses cloud quantum simulators (SV1 and DM1) rather than physical QPUs, with physical QPU randomness planned as a near-term extension. Measurable benchmarks are identified as priority future work, and the paper provides qualitative deployment evidence from live events.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Quantum randomness extraction uses two independent weak random sources to produce near-perfect random bits. A Toeplitz two-source extractor is a classical algorithm that multiplies a Toeplitz matrix with input vectors to extract randomness. SV1 and DM1 are quantum simulators that emulate quantum circuits on classical hardware. The system combines these with an AI conversational bot to make quantum outputs understandable to non-experts.

## [FedSteer: Corrective Projections and Caching for Stale Gradients in FL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.10124) ⭐️ 7.0/10

FedSteer introduces a method that projects stale gradients onto a low-dimensional subspace constructed from cached recent client updates, effectively steering them toward the current global objective. This addresses the critical problem of gradient staleness in federated learning, which can destabilize training when client participation is skewed, potentially improving robustness and accuracy in real-world FL deployments. FedSteer uses a selective caching strategy to identify a representative client subset for subspace construction, reducing server memory overhead. Experiments show accuracy gains of over 7% and prevention of performance collapse in challenging scenarios.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Federated learning (FL) trains a shared model across decentralized clients without sharing raw data. A common challenge is that clients may not participate consistently, leading to stale model updates that can degrade performance. Existing methods reuse stale updates to reduce variance, but extreme staleness can destabilize training.

Traditional appearance descriptors are static and frame-independent, often requiring heavy computation, which leads many real-time systems to omit appearance cues entirely. Polycepta reformulates appearance modeling as a recursive estimation problem, similar to how Kalman filters estimate motion states, allowing appearance to be refined over time.

This research challenges the assumption that more complex AI models always outperform, showing that combining classical value investing principles with simple models can yield better risk-adjusted returns, which is significant for quantitative fund managers and systematic traders. ASALT introduces adaptive observation-level and state-level adapters that map target-domain observations and global states into a shared embedding space, enabling lateral transfer learning in multi-agent reinforcement learning even when source and target domains have mismatched observation and state dimensionalities. This work addresses a key limitation in multi-agent transfer learning by removing the requirement for identical state-space dimensions across domains, potentially enabling more practical and flexible knowledge reuse in heterogeneous multi-agent systems. ASALT uses a two-block Transformer encoder as the state adapter to encode global states, and its effectiveness depends on the degree of mismatch between source and target domains. Experimental results show it surpasses existing baselines in sample efficiency and global return in cooperative settings, and mitigates negative transfer.

**Background**: Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) trains multiple agents to achieve collaborative or competitive goals. Transfer learning in MARL aims to reuse knowledge from a source domain to accelerate learning in a target domain, but most prior methods require identical observation and state dimensionalities, limiting their applicability. Lateral transfer refers to transferring knowledge across different tasks or domains without a hierarchy. ASALT relaxes this constraint by learning adapters that align state representations.

## [0-1 Law for Random Colored Digraphs via MLN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23715) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper proves that for random colored digraphs defined by a Markov logic network with weights scaled by 1/n, a 0-1 law for first-order logic holds, and the limit probability is independent of the weights. This result advances the theoretical understanding of probabilistic relational models, showing that scaling weights can simplify asymptotic behavior, which has implications for inference in large domains. The paper considers a language with a property P(x) and a relation R(x,y), and an MLN where every Boolean combination of P and R is a soft constraint. Without scaling, seven qualitatively different cases emerge, including phase transitions.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: A Markov logic network (MLN) is a probabilistic relational model that combines first-order logic with Markov networks, assigning weights to formulas to define a probability distribution over possible worlds. A 0-1 law states that for any first-order sentence, its probability tends to either 0 or 1 as the domain size grows. This work studies the asymptotic behavior of such probabilities under different weight scaling schemes.

Researchers extended a neural-network-based parametric model reduction method by incorporating a variational autoencoder to predict turbulent flow around multiple vehicle geometries with varying shapes. This work aims to significantly reduce computational cost in aerodynamic simulations, which is critical for industrial vehicle design where many geometry variations must be evaluated. The method uses a variational autoencoder to create a compact latent representation and evaluates reconstruction accuracy of vortex generation across different spatial and temporal scales, focusing on flow near the vehicle rear end.

## [Image Transformations in Latent Space for Histopathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24430) ⭐️ 6.0/10

This paper investigates how classical image transformations affect latent space embeddings from histopathology-specific encoders (Lunit, Bioptimus) and a general encoder (Meta), finding that embeddings are robust but not fully invariant to transformations. Understanding transformation invariance in latent space is crucial for improving data augmentation strategies and downstream task performance in medical AI, especially for histopathology classification. The study used hematoxylin/eosin-stained image tiles from colorectal tissue and TCGA datasets, comparing original and transformed embeddings against random embeddings to assess variance.

**Background**: In histopathology, neural networks encode images into a latent space to reduce complexity and improve classification performance. Encoder networks can be pretrained on general datasets like ImageNet or on histopathology-specific data. This work examines how well these encoders handle label-irrelevant transformations, which is important for robust feature extraction.

## [MedPCFM: Flow Matching Boosts Medical Point Cloud Completion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24433) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers introduce PCFM, a flow matching method backed by Point Transformer V3 (PTv3), for medical point cloud completion, achieving state-of-the-art generative performance on SkullFix, SkullBreak, and Mandibular Defect datasets with fewer sampling steps than diffusion models. This work demonstrates that flow matching can outperform diffusion models in medical point cloud completion while being more efficient, potentially enabling faster and more accurate anatomical reconstruction for clinical workflows. PCFM with PTv3 achieves up to 7× speed-up over a PVCNN backbone, and the study shows consistent gains with higher point resolution and informative trade-offs across model scales.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Medical point cloud completion aims to reconstruct missing parts of 3D anatomical structures from partial point clouds, which is important for surgical planning and diagnosis. Flow matching is a generative modeling technique that learns a continuous transformation from noise to data, often requiring fewer steps than diffusion models. Point Transformer V3 (PTv3) is a state-of-the-art architecture for point cloud processing that simplifies neighbor search and expands receptive field.

## [RetiSEM: Causal Modeling for Fragmented Biomedical Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.24488) ⭐️ 6.0/10

RetiSEM introduces a domain-constrained structural equation modeling framework that organizes variables into biologically informed blocks, applies forbidden-edge constraints, and decomposes pathway effects into total, natural direct, and natural indirect effects for causal graph recovery and mediation analysis from fragmented biomedical data. This framework enables causal inference from incomplete multimodal biomedical data, which is common in clinical practice, and provides interpretable mediation analysis to uncover how variables influence outcomes through intermediate pathways. RetiSEM was evaluated on ten synthetic benchmarks and a real-world fragmented dataset combining NHANES clinical variables with retinal representations, achieving lower structural error and higher causal accuracy than unconstrained baselines.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Structural equation modeling (SEM) is a statistical technique for testing causal relationships using a combination of observed and latent variables. Mediation analysis decomposes the total effect of an exposure on an outcome into direct and indirect effects through a mediator. Fragmented biomedical data refers to datasets where variables from different modalities (e.g., clinical, molecular, imaging) are not jointly observed for all subjects, posing challenges for traditional causal inference methods.

## [EMFusion: Conditional Diffusion for Multivariate EMF Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.15067) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose EMFusion, a conditional diffusion model that forecasts multivariate narrow-band electromagnetic field (EMF) levels with uncertainty awareness, outperforming baselines by 23.85% in CRPS and 13.93% in NRMSE. This work enables proactive network planning and compliance monitoring by capturing inter-operator and inter-frequency variations, moving beyond simple univariate wideband forecasts. EMFusion uses a residual U-Net backbone with cross-attention to integrate contextual factors like time and holidays, and treats forecasting as an inpainting task for temporal coherence.

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Diffusion models are generative models that learn to reverse a noise-adding process to generate data. Conditional diffusion models incorporate additional inputs (e.g., time of day) to guide generation. EMF forecasting traditionally uses univariate wideband methods, but multivariate narrow-band forecasting is needed for detailed network planning.

A new framework integrates vehicle sensor data with environmental signals (road quality, weather, traffic, driver behavior) for predictive maintenance, achieving a 2.6-point F1 improvement on synthetic data and AUC-ROC 0.973 on the AI4I 2020 benchmark. This work demonstrates that incorporating external environmental context significantly improves predictive maintenance accuracy for connected vehicles, potentially reducing unexpected breakdowns and improving fleet reliability. The framework was validated on real-world telemetry from five vehicles across three countries (India, Germany, Brazil) with 992 trips and 11 service events, achieving 100% detection with mean MAE of 12.2 days. Edge-based inference reduces latency from 3.5 seconds to under 1.0 second.

Researchers used AI-based speech processing to analyze acoustic features of teacher talk in 36 team-teaching sessions, revealing systematic differences in loudness variation across teacher experience, student cohorts, and learning task design. ## [Hybrid Neuro-Symbolic Framework for Irony Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.16845) ⭐️ 6.0/10

Researchers propose the Robust Dual-Signal (RDS) Fusion framework, a hybrid neuro-symbolic architecture that compresses Chain-of-Thought reasoning trajectories without supervised fine-tuning, achieving 78.1% accuracy on TweetEval and outperforming supervised models on iSarcasm. This work demonstrates that small-scale LLMs can match fine-tuned performance on irony detection without expensive supervised fine-tuning, offering a cost-effective solution for social media text analysis. It also advances neuro-symbolic methods by showing that compressed CoT reasoning can effectively filter hallucinations. On the imbalanced iSarcasm dataset, the frozen CoT pipeline filters 22.5% of out-of-distribution hallucinations, achieving a few-shot Macro F1 of 0.6726 and Ironic F1 of 0.4821. Statistical ablation confirms that the concurrent fusion yields a significant improvement over the standalone baseline (p=0.005).

rss · ArXiv ML (cs.AI) · Jun 24, 04:00

**Background**: Irony detection in social media is challenging due to noisy, user-generated text and the literal bias of small-scale LLMs. Chain-of-Thought reasoning helps models reason step-by-step but often requires supervised fine-tuning. Neuro-symbolic approaches combine neural networks with symbolic reasoning to improve robustness. The TweetEval benchmark includes seven Twitter classification tasks, and iSarcasm is a heavily imbalanced irony detection dataset.
